It’s been going on for maybe about four months, around when things with my spouse started getting more tense—more arguments than before. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s probably when I noticed the pattern starting.\n\nDoctor: Has this been interfering with your work or daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m managing, but not as well as I’d like. Like sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking, yoga… even tennis with a friend. Those usually make me feel like myself, and lately I just kind of… don’t reach for them. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most days lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think it’s most days, no. I have moments where I feel kind of low or flat, but I wouldn’t want to say I’m sad all the time. It’s more like… I’m not as bright, maybe? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure… I mean, my sleep hasn’t felt perfect, but I don’t know if I’d call it “trouble sleeping.” Some nights are just kind of off, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really been tracking. I mean, sometimes I lie there a bit and my mind’s not totally quiet, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Maybe normal? I’m not sure what counts as difficulty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think unusually anxious, no… I mean, I worry about normal things, but I wouldn’t say it’s been out of control or anything. At least, I don’t think so. Is that okay—like, is that what you mean by unusually worried?\n\nDoctor: Did this tiredness start after a specific stressful event or major life change?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, kind of. It’s been going on for maybe about four months, around when things with my spouse started getting more tense—more arguments than before. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s probably when I noticed the pattern starting.\n\nDoctor: Has this been interfering with your work or daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m managing, but not as well as I’d like. I’ve fallen behind on my certificate coursework, and things like bills, meals, and house stuff kind of pile up because I make plans and then don’t really follow through consistently.\n\nMy spouse has had to step in more at home, which I feel bad about. And I’ve pulled back some from friends and art things I usually care about. I don’t know if that counts as “interfering,” but it’s definitely been harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I feel bad when I don’t keep up with things the way I should, but I wouldn’t say I feel worthless. Maybe guilty sometimes in a normal way? I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. It’s not like I can’t think at all, but I get kind of stuck. Like I’ll have my planning app open and I’ll just sit there trying to decide whether I should do a class assignment, pay a bill, or start dinner, and somehow I don’t choose any of them for a while. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. I’ve had off days, definitely, but “down or depressed nearly every day” feels stronger than what I’d say. I’m not trying to minimize it—I just don’t think that’s been the main thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During this past month, was there any period of at least two weeks when you felt down, sad, or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there was a full two-week stretch like that, no. I mean, I’ve had some sad or kind of empty moments, but not most of the day nearly every day. At least… not that I noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the night, do you have trouble falling back asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I do wake up sometimes, but I haven’t really paid close attention to whether it takes me a long time to fall back asleep. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it hopeless, exactly. I’ve had moments where I feel unsure or kind of discouraged, but not like… no future or anything. At least I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
